## Item checkpoint 3/60 — `def-zariski-cotangent-space-point`

- **Claim/convention:** for any scheme point $x$, defines the intrinsic cotangent space as $\mathfrak m_x/\mathfrak m_x^2$ over $\kappa(x)=\mathcal O_{X,x}/\mathfrak m_x$; states that no relative-cotangent identification is implicit. The residue field acts because $\mathfrak m_x$ annihilates this quotient.
- **Sources:** Milne, *Algebraic Geometry*, §4f, Proposition 4.29 and §4.30, printed pp. 90–91, opened in full. Milne proves the dual-number/derivation/cotangent identification when the residue field is the ground field; that is only the rational closed-point specialization used here. Stacks Project, Varieties §33.16 (tag `0B28`), lines 43–47, distinguishes the relative cotangent space $\Omega_{X/S,x}\otimes\kappa(x)$; no equality with $\mathfrak m_x/\mathfrak m_x^2$ is claimed for general nonclosed points.
- **Examined dependency:** `def-residue-field-scheme-point`, which defines $\kappa(x)$ as the quotient of the local ring by its maximal ideal.
- **Repair:** none. Added the scalar-action verification and calculated examples at the origin and generic point of $\operatorname{Spec}k[t]$ (cotangent dimensions one and zero).
- **Contract/checks:** strict contract has no proof steps and records empty-scheme, zero, one, degenerate, choice, endpoint and iff dispositions. Explicit-path precheck: pass (0 proof-bearing items); rendercheck: pass (1 file); strict contract: pass (1/1).

## Item checkpoint 4/60 — `lem-local-dimension-reduced-variety-components`

- **Claim/convention:** Under AC, for reduced classical finite-type `X` over algebraically closed `k` and closed `x`, `dim O_{X,x}` is the maximum of `dim X_i` over irreducible components containing `x`. Components not containing `x` do not contribute.
- **Source passages reread:** Milne, *Algebraic Geometry*, §3c notes 3.13–3.14, printed p. 63: primes of the local ring correspond to irreducible closed subsets through the point, and local minimal primes correspond to components; this is structural context, not the dimension equality. Milne, §4i Corollary 4.45, printed pp. 96–97, concerns regularity and was checked to prevent attributing the dimension statement to it. Milne, *Algebraic Geometry Ch. 10 supplement*, §§10.54–10.56, printed pp. 15–16 (web PDF pp. 14–15): chain/Krull dimension and dimension of an irreducible algebraic scheme. The irreducible closed-point equality used in the proof is instead the exact published supplier `lem-dimension-local-ring-codimension-closure`.
- **Examined direct dependencies:** `def-axiom-of-choice`; `def-coordinate-ring-affine-algebraic-set`; `lem-classical-variety-noetherian-components`; `lem-dimension-local-ring-codimension-closure`; `thm-classical-affine-nullstellensatz-correspondence`; `thm-local-ring-affine-variety-localization`; `thm-prime-spectrum-of-a-localisation-bijection`. Their statements and relevant proofs were read. The affine chart route identifies `O_X,x` with `A_m`; component ideals are prime; prime chains in `A_m` contract to chains in `A` under primes inside `m`. Irreducibility places the closed set of the first chain prime inside one component through `x`, and quotient localization identifies the component local ring. Every component quotient chain lifts back, giving the reverse inequality.

## Item checkpoint 6/60 — lem-zero-scheme-of-line-bundle-section

- **Claim/convention:** For any scheme X, invertible (locally free rank-one) O_X-module L, and global section s, the local quotients Spec(A_i/(f_i)) from affine trivializations glue with their quotient maps to a zero closed subscheme, canonical up to unique isomorphism over X. Quotient by (f_i) itself, not its radical; no nonzerodivisor or reducedness condition; unit coefficient gives empty chart and zero section gives all of X.
- **Source read in full:** Vakil, Foundations of Algebraic Geometry Classes 51–52, §3.9 Corollary 3.9, printed p. 10 / PDF page 10, lines 436–445, URL https://virtualmath1.stanford.edu/~vakil/0506-216/216class5152.pdf. The corollary says each section gives a closed subscheme, then asserts general members are smooth; Exercise 3.10 asks for the Bertini proof. This passage does not prove the local quotient-and-gluing construction; that proof is supplied in the item.
- **Examined direct dependencies:** def-module-on-ringed-space; def-section-restriction-and-global-section; thm-localisation-commutes-with-quotients; thm-gluing-affine-schemes; lem-morphism-schemes-local-on-source-target; lem-quotient-spectrum-map-is-closed; def-closed-immersion-schemes; lem-closed-immersion-local-on-target. All are published; relevant statements and the quotient, gluing and local closed-immersion proofs were read.
- **Scaffold repair:** Added the three omitted published direct prerequisites: quotient spectra are homeomorphic onto their closed zero sets; compatible scheme maps glue; closed immersions are local on the target. Updated item frontmatter, manifest statement/dependencies/strategy, and coverage source locator/support/proof plan. No new local supplier or cross-batch edge; dependency level remains 0.
- **Proof:** On each affine trivialization the section has coefficient f_i; the quotient spectrum is homeomorphic onto V(f_i), and localization gives the local quotient maps on distinguished opens, so the quotient morphism is a closed immersion by definition. Transition generators differ by a unit, so their coefficient ideals agree on affine overlap refinements; localization identifies restrictions and identity-on-functions maps satisfy the cocycle. Scheme gluing gives the zero scheme and morphism gluing gives its map to X. Closed immersion locality then proves the global map is a closed immersion. Common affine refinements prove independence and unique isomorphism over X. The calculation (k[epsilon]/(epsilon^3))/(epsilon^2) ≅ k[epsilon]/(epsilon^2) exhibits a retained nilpotent.
- **Contract/checks:** Strict contract: 8 exact dependency excerpts, 5 derivations, all eight boundary axes; PASS (0 errors, 0 warnings). Explicit-path precheck: PASS; explicit-path rendercheck: PASS. Recomputed item-dependency-levels: PASS (924 items, 60 pages, max level 18).
- **Decision:** Refreshed non-owner pair scope to sufficient; recorded repaired, confidence 1, against all eight direct dependencies in research/frontier-36-complete-step3b-review-lem-zero-scheme-of-line-bundle-section.json.

## Item checkpoint 7/60 — def-linear-system-base-locus

- **Claim/convention:** Over algebraically closed k, a linear system on a k-scheme X with invertible sheaf L is a nonzero finite-dimensional k-subspace W of Γ(X,L). Its parameter space is P(W), the lines in W with the basis-independent projective Zariski topology. The base locus is the intersection of the underlying zero sets of its sections; base-point-free means empty base locus. A property holds for a general member when it holds on every parameter in some nonempty Zariski-open subset. For X embedded in projective space, degree-one and degree-e homogeneous forms give the hyperplane and degree-e systems.
- **Source passages read in full:** Vakil, Foundations of Algebraic Geometry Classes 51–52, §3.9 Corollary 3.9, printed p. 10 / PDF p. 10, lines 437–441. It treats a base-point-free finite-dimensional space of sections and general sections as projective parameters, then states each section gives a closed subscheme; Exercise 3.10 leaves the Bertini proof. Arapura, Notes on Basic Algebraic Geometry, §5.4, printed pp. 38–39 / PDF pp. 38–39, lines 1689–1721. It identifies hyperplanes from linear forms modulo nonzero scalars with the dual projective space, states the smooth-hyperplane conclusion on a nonempty open, and supplies an incidence proof under its smoothness and characteristic-zero hypotheses. Those hypotheses/proof are not generalized here.

## Item checkpoint 8/60 — def-tangent-cone-point

- **Claim/convention:** For a point $x$ of a locally Noetherian scheme $X$, define the scheme-theoretic tangent cone as $\operatorname{Spec}(\operatorname{gr}_{\mathfrak m_x}\mathcal O_{X,x})$ with its canonical $\kappa(x)$-algebra structure from degree zero. Use $\operatorname{Cone}_x(X)$ to avoid colliding with the immediately preceding item’s notation $C_xX$ for the cotangent space. The reduction may equal the full cone, but can differ.

Milne, *Algebraic Geometry* Ch. 10 supplement, §f Definitions 10.69–10.71, printed pp. 18–19/PDF pp. 17–18: Definition 10.71 is specifically for a $k$-rational point and sets the cone to $\operatorname{Spm}(\operatorname{gr}\mathcal O_P)$; this is the rational-point specialization, not an explicit statement for every scheme point. Stacks Project §27.7, Definitions 27.7.1–27.7.2 (tag 062P), complete section, lines 18–34: defines the cone of a graded algebra by relative Spec when degree zero is the base and retains the grading. The arbitrary-point formulation here uses the associated graded local ring as a graded $\kappa(x)$-algebra; no rationality or coefficient-field choice is assumed.

- **Calculation:** At $D_k=\operatorname{Spec}(k[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^2))$ at $(\epsilon)$, elements $a+b\epsilon$ with $a\ne0$ are units, so the local ring is the dual-numbers ring. The graded pieces are $k$ in degree zero, $k\epsilon$ in degree one, and zero from degree two onward; the degree-one class squares to zero. Thus the cone is $D_k$ and its reduction is $\operatorname{Spec}k$, with the same one-point space and different structure sheaf. If $\mathfrak m_x=0$, all positive graded pieces vanish and the cone is $\operatorname{Spec}\kappa(x)$.

## Item checkpoint 12/60 — `lem-tangent-cone-initial-ideal-presentation`

- **Claim/convention:** For any field $k$, finite $n$, $P=k[t_1,\ldots,t_n]$, $\mathfrak q=(t_1,\ldots,t_n)$ and arbitrary ideal $I\subseteq\mathfrak q$, let $X=\operatorname{Spec}(P/I)$ and let $x$ be the rational origin. The graded $k$-algebra map $P/\operatorname{in}_{\mathfrak q}(I)\to\operatorname{gr}_{\mathfrak m_x}\mathcal O_{X,x}$ is an isomorphism, where the initial ideal uses the lowest nonzero homogeneous part of **every** nonzero element of $I$. Thus the scheme-theoretic cone is $\operatorname{Spec}(P/\operatorname{in}_{\mathfrak q}(I))$ with nilpotents retained. For $I=(f)$, $f\ne0$, $\operatorname{in}_{\mathfrak q}(I)=(f_{\min})$; initials of a selected generating list may fail to generate the full initial ideal. No radicality, irreducibility, or perfectness is assumed.

## Item checkpoint 13/60 — `lem-tangent-vectors-as-dual-number-points`

- **Claim/convention:** For an arbitrary $k$-scheme and a $k$-rational point, the intrinsic tangent space is naturally the based dual-number-point fibre and is canonically identified with $\operatorname{Der}_k(\mathcal O_{X,x},k)$ for the residue action. The assertion is restricted to rational points; no identification is asserted for a nonrational point.

Item 4.30(a)–(d), printed p. 92 / PDF p. 91 (web lines 4922–4969), records the affine-variety fibre, derivation, localization and cotangent identifications. Milne AG10 §f, 10.60, printed pp. 16–17 / PDF pp. 16–17 (web lines 904–917), makes the same claim for algebraic schemes over an arbitrary field at points with residue field $k$. ## Item checkpoint 15/60 — `def-multiplicity-hypersurface-point`

- **Claim/convention:** For any field $k$, finite $n$, rational $a\in k^n$, and nonzero $f\in k[X_1,\ldots,X_n]$ with $f(a)=0$, multiplicity is the least degree of a nonzero homogeneous summand in $f(a+t)$. ## Item checkpoint 19/60 — `lem-tangent-cone-linear-span-tangent-space`

- **Claim/convention:** For a locally Noetherian $k$-scheme and a $k$-rational point $x$, the cotangent space $C_xX=\mathfrak m_x/\mathfrak m_x^2$ is finite-dimensional. The canonical multiplication map $S_k(C_xX)\to\operatorname{gr}_{\mathfrak m_x}\mathcal O_{X,x}$ is graded-surjective and is the identity in degree one. Consequently the scheme-theoretic tangent cone embeds as a closed subscheme of $\mathbb A(T_xX)$ and is contained in no proper linear closed subscheme. Nilpotents are retained. The zero cotangent-space case is treated explicitly; the dual-numbers example has cone $\operatorname{Spec}k[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^2)$, whose linear span is $\mathbb A^1$.
- **Source passages read and qualified:** Milne, *Algebraic Geometry, Chapter 10 supplement*, §f, Definitions 10.69–10.71, printed pp. 18–19 / PDF pp. 17–18 / web lines 1020–1039; Chapter 10 introduction, PDF lines 0–12, states the arbitrary-field and nilpotent setting. Proposition 10.70 asserts the associated-graded presentation but sends its proof to 4.34. Milne, *Algebraic Geometry* v6.10, §4g, Proposition 4.34 and preceding tangent-cone discussion, printed pp. 93–94 / PDF pp. 92–93 / web lines 5016–5071, gives the complete classical argument and a generator-initial warning. Its global algebraically-closed-field convention and classical algebraic-variety setup limit its scope; neither source is treated as proving this item's general scheme claim. The item proves the map directly by factoring products modulo $\mathfrak m_x^{d+1}$.
- **Examined direct dependencies:** `def-tangent-cone-point`; `def-zariski-tangent-space-point`; `def-locally-noetherian-and-noetherian-scheme`; `def-prime-and-maximal-ideals`; `def-noetherian-ring`; `def-noetherian-module`; `def-left-right-and-two-sided-ideal`; `def-submodule`; `def-localisation-at-a-prime-ideal`; `thm-localisation-at-a-prime-is-local`; `thm-stalk-structure-sheaf-prime-localization`; `def-dual-numbers-scheme`; `def-symmetric-algebra-of-a-vector-space`; `thm-universal-property-of-the-symmetric-algebra`. Read their definitions, statements, and relevant complete proofs. The Noetherian affine chart gives a finite generating list for the fixed prime; localization carries it to generators of the stalk maximal ideal. The symmetric-algebra universal property gives the canonical map, while the direct degreewise argument proves surjectivity.

## Item checkpoint 24/60 — `ex-orthogonal-and-symplectic-tangent-matrices`

- **Claim/conventions:** For $n\ge1$, the orthogonal and special orthogonal affine schemes are cut out in $M_n$ by $A^{\mathsf T}A=I_n$, with the additional equation $\det A=1$ for the special orthogonal scheme; when $\operatorname{char}k\ne2$, both identity tangents are the skew-symmetric matrices. In every characteristic, the symplectic affine scheme is cut out in $M_{2n}$ by $A^{\mathsf T}JA=J$ and $\det A=1$, where $J=\begin{pmatrix}0&I_n\\-I_n&0\end{pmatrix}$. Its identity tangent consists exactly of $\begin{pmatrix}P&Q\\R&-P^{\mathsf T}\end{pmatrix}$ with $Q,R$ symmetric and has dimension $n(2n+1)$. The item explicitly makes no global group-scheme dimension or smoothness claim.
- **Source passages re-read in full:** Milne, *Algebraic Geometry* v6.10, §4j Example 4.48, printed pp.97–98/PDF pp.96–97, web lines 5245–5262 (`turn21view0`): under characteristic not two, the classical $O_n$ and $SO_n$ tangent spaces are the skew matrices. Exercise 4-6, printed p.99/PDF p.98, lines 5315–5322 (`turn21view1`), is only a prompt for the symplectic tangent calculation and group dimension. The complete official solution 4-6, printed p.222/PDF p.221, lines 13403–13422 (`turn21view2`), derives $B^{\mathsf T}J+JB=0$, its block form, and the count $2n^2+n$. Milne's book-wide algebraically-closed-field convention was rechecked at printed p.5. The solution does not separately check the determinant equation to first order, and the classical book convention does not establish the arbitrary-field scheme statement; both are proved directly here. No source proof of global group dimension is used.
- **Examined direct dependencies:** `lem-tangent-vectors-as-dual-number-points`; `def-dual-numbers-scheme`; `def-scheme-over-base`; `thm-affine-scheme-ring-anti-equivalence`; `thm-universal-property-of-a-polynomial-ring-on-a-family`; `thm-quotient-ring-universal-property`; `def-matrices-over-a-commutative-ring`; `def-ring-matrix-product-identity-and-transpose`; `def-trace-of-a-square-matrix-over-a-commutative-ring`; `def-determinant-of-a-square-matrix`; `cor-dimensions-of-matrix-and-linear-map-spaces`. Re-read all relevant statements and complete supplier arguments. The dual-number lemma applies to these $k$-rational identities; the affine, polynomial, and quotient correspondences produce exactly the matrices over $D$ satisfying the defining equations; matrix operations, trace, Leibniz determinant, and the matrix-space dimension corollary support the entrywise computations.
- **Scaffold repair and proof:** Made each tangent calculation's equation-defined affine-scheme convention explicit and qualified the classical source in the item reference and coverage record. Step 1.1 uses the dual-number tangent lemma and affine coordinate-ring correspondences to write $A=I+\epsilon B$. Step 2.1 expands $A^{\mathsf T}A=I$; characteristic not two forces diagonal entries to zero and the off-diagonal entries to be negatives. Directly from the Leibniz determinant formula, the identity permutation contributes $1+\epsilon\sum_i b_{ii}$ and each nonidentity permutation term has at least two epsilon factors. Thus $\det(I+\epsilon B)=1+\epsilon\operatorname{tr}B$, so the special orthogonal equation adds no condition. Step 3.1 expands the symplectic equation into the four block equations, which are equivalent to symmetry of $Q,R$ and $S=-P^{\mathsf T}$. Then $\operatorname{tr}B=\operatorname{tr}P-\operatorname{tr}(P^{\mathsf T})=0$ in every characteristic, so its determinant equation adds no first-order condition. Step 4.1 parametrizes each symmetric block by its diagonal and upper-triangular entries and counts $n^2+2(n(n+1)/2)=n(2n+1)$. The characteristic-two $O_1$ example has arbitrary tangent coefficient $b$ from $(1+\epsilon b)^2=1$, while $SO_1$ forces $b=0$; this verifies the orthogonal characteristic qualifier. The proof establishes both equation directions, the identity/zero vector, $n=1$, and the restriction against global conclusions.

## Item checkpoint 25/60 — `cor-hypersurface-singular-locus-gradient`

- **Claim/conventions:** Under AC, let $k$ be algebraically closed, $n\ge1$, and $f\in k[t_1,\ldots,t_n]$ nonconstant and squarefree. For each classical closed point $a\in X(k)$ of the reduced hypersurface $X=V(f)$, singularity is equivalent to the vanishing of every formal first partial; thus $X_{\mathrm{sing}}=V(f,\partial_1f,\ldots,\partial_nf)$ as subsets of $k^n$. The actual affine scheme is $\operatorname{Spec}(k[t]/(f))$. Because squarefreeness makes $(f)$ radical, radicalization is redundant in this scope; a non-squarefree equation can define a different scheme after radicalization, as shown by $t^2$ versus $t$.

## Item checkpoint 27/60 — `lem-smooth-map-tangent-surjectivity-criterion`

- **Claim/conventions:** Under AC, let $k$ be algebraically closed and $X,Y$ smooth classical varieties with locally standard-smooth finite-type $k$-scheme structures. For a finite-type $k$-morphism $f:X\to Y$ and each classical closed point $x$ with $y=f(x)$, smoothness at $x$ (standard smoothness after shrinking) is equivalent to surjectivity of $d_xf$. Closed classical points have residue field $k$; no generic-point tangent convention is used.

Hence every member; hence the general member, is not smooth: the arbitrary-linear-system Bertini statement fails in characteristic p. - **Claim/scope.** For any field $k$: $V=\operatorname{Spec}(k[x,y,z]/(xy,yz,zx))$ has $k$-rational points exactly the three coordinate axes of $\mathbb A^3_k$, and $T_oV\cong k^3$ has dimension $3$ at the origin $o$. For every finite list $g_1,\ldots,g_r\in k[u,v]$ and every $k$-rational point $q$ of $W=\operatorname{Spec}(k[u,v]/(g_1,\ldots,g_r))$, $T_qW\cong\ker J(q)\subseteq k^2$ so $\dim_kT_qW\le2$. Hence no isomorphism $V\to W$ over $k$ exists; in particular $V$ is not isomorphic to Milne's $W=\operatorname{Spec}(k[u,v]/(uv(u-v)))$, the union of the three distinct lines $u=0$, $v=0$, $u=v$ (distinctness witnessed by $(0,1)$, $(1,0)$, $(1,1)$).

An isomorphism $V\to W$ would have to send the singular point $o$ to the singular point $o$ and map $T_o(V)$ isomorphically onto $T_o(W)$. But $V=V(XY,YZ,XZ)$, and so $T_o(V)$ has dimension 3, whereas $T_o(W)$ has dimension 2. Therefore, $V$ and $W$ are not isomorphic." - **Claim/scope.** Let $k$ be any field and $a\in k$; $C=\operatorname{Spec}(k[x,y]/(y-x^2))\subseteq\mathbb A^2_k$, $P=(a,a^2)$. The Jacobian row of the single equation $f=y-x^2$ at $P$ is $(-2a,1)$, and the Jacobian-kernel isomorphism gives $T_PC\cong\ker(-2a,1)=\{(u,2au):u\in k\}=k\cdot(1,2a)$, one-dimensional in every characteristic; in characteristic $2$ the row is $(0,1)$, the kernel $k\cdot(1,0)=\{(u,0)\}$ and the tangent line $Y=a^2$. In the translated coordinates $(X-a,Y-a^2)=(u,v)$ the tangent line is $v=2au$, i.e. $Y-a^2=2a(X-a)$. The maximal ideal is principal, $\mathfrak m=(\bar x-a)$, generated by the local parameter $t=\bar x-a$, and the class $[t]$ is a $k$-basis of $\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2\cong k$; the localization lemma (r=1) gives $C_PC\cong\mathfrak m/\mathfrak m^2$, so $T_PC\cong\operatorname{Hom}_k(k,k)\cong k$ is one-dimensional as well.

- **Item:** `items/thm-bertini-smooth-hyperplane-section.md`, new file, 43 deps, stored level 9 = computed 9, canonical proof steps 1.1-9.1, `## Source qualification` present.
- **Claim/scope (authored).** AC; $k$ algebraically closed of characteristic $0$; $X$ a smooth finite-type $k$-scheme admitting a locally closed immersion into a projective space; $L$ invertible on $X$; $W\subseteq\Gamma(X,L)$ nonzero finite-dimensional with $\dim_kW=r+1$, base locus $\operatorname{Bs}(W)$, $X^\circ=X\smallsetminus\operatorname{Bs}(W)$, so $\mathbf P(W)=\mathbf P^r_k$. Then there is a nonempty Zariski-open $U\subseteq\mathbf P(W)$ such that for every closed point $[s]\in U$ (equivalently every classical parameter, by the dictionary) the closed subscheme $Z(s)\cap X^\circ$ is smooth over $k$; $U$ contains classical parameters. In particular, for a fixed locally closed immersion $X\hookrightarrow\mathbf P^N_k$ with $X\ne\varnothing$, the hyperplane system has empty base locus and general scheme-theoretic hyperplane sections $X\times_{\mathbf P^N_k}V_+(F)$ are smooth over $k$. No irreducibility, connectedness, dimension or nonemptiness of $X$ or of the members is asserted; $X^\circ$ may be empty.
- **Source passages read.** Vakil Classes 51-52 §3.9 Corollary (base-point-free Bertini, printed p.10) with §3.10-3.11 (old Bertini as corollary; positive-characteristic failure), and Arapura §5.4 Theorem 5.4.5 with its incidence/local-product proof and Lemma 5.4.4 (printed pp. 38-39). Both sources assume base-point-free systems (Vakil) or treat hyperplane sections (Arapura); the item's removal of the base locus and the componentwise route are recorded as the authored divergence in `## Source qualification`.
- **Examined dependencies (43).** All frontmatter deps re-read or statement-checked: the in-run suppliers `lem-linear-system-incidence-is-smooth` (clauses (1)-(4) plus its step-2.1 construction of $I$ as a locally closed subscheme of $X^\circ\times_k\mathbf P^r_k$), `thm-generic-smoothness-characteristic-zero` (clause 2 with nonempty smooth pure-dimension closed-point fibres), `lem-regular-point-lies-on-one-component`, `thm-regular-equals-smooth-over-perfect-field`, `def-linear-system-base-locus` (index convention $\dim_kW=r+1$ here versus $\dim_kW=r$ in its own $\mathbf P(W)\cong\mathbf P^{r-1}$ sentence), plus the published dictionary, component, Noetherian, separability, projective-space and classical-point suppliers.
- **Scaffold repair.** 5 → 43 deps; scaffold strategy superseded by the authored route but its two load-bearing claims are kept: (i) the whole base-locus-free part of every general member is proved smooth (not merely a dense part); (ii) the finite open intersection is taken over the finitely many incidence components, with nondominant components removed via image closures. The scaffold's bare "smooth incidence has finitely many components" step was expanded into reducedness of the incidence (regular local rings are domains), disjointness of components, classical-variety typing via the affine-overlap criterion, and separatedness of $I$ from the step-2.1 closed-subscheme construction.
- **Proof route (canonical steps 1.1-9.1).** 1.1 setup/incidence; 1.2 $\mathbf P^r_k$ is a nonempty irreducible classical projective variety via $I_+(\mathbf P^r_k)=(0)$ (nonzero polynomial has a nonvanishing point) and the polynomial ring domain; 1.3 $I$ regular, reduced, Noetherian; 1.4 separatedness of $X$, $X^\circ\times_k\mathbf P^r_k$ and $I$; 1.5 nondominant components and image-closure complements; 1.6 hyperplane system nonzero with empty base locus; 2.1 finite disjoint open integral component decomposition $I=\bigsqcup Z_i$ (components also open); 2.2 hyperplane sections identified with $Z(s)$ locally; 3.1 dictionary typing of $Z_i$ and $\mathbf P(W)$ and of $p_i$; 4.1 generic smoothness on dominant components (nonempty open $V_i$, closed-point fibres nonempty smooth pure dim $\dim Z_i-r$); 5.1 finite intersection $U\ne\varnothing$ by irreducibility; 6.1 fibre $p^{-1}([s])$ covered by the open pieces $F\cap Z_i=p_i^{-1}([s])$, each smooth or empty, hence smooth by locality; 7.1 incidence clause (2) identifies it with $Z(s)\cap X^\circ$; 8.1 classical parameters of $U$ are nonempty (affine charts + density of closed points); 8.2 hyperplane clause; 9.1 boundary/AC/iff dispositions.
- **Choice.** AC declared as [F1]; traced in step 9.1 through [F3], [F4], [F5], [F7], [F10], [F13], [F14], [F24], [F38], [F39] and the separatedness chain [F18]-[F21]; the finite choices of charts, bases, components and representatives add no choice principle.
